Esse achado foi corrigido nesta sessao:
- src/modules/identity/ports/identity-store.ts ganhou updateConditional(item, expected: {count, resetAt}) - PutCommand com ConditionExpression comparando count/resetAt.
- src/modules/identity/persistence/dynamodb-identity-store.ts implementa updateConditional com ConditionExpression real.
- test/unit/identity/in-memory-store.ts (fake usado nos testes) implementa a mesma semantica condicional.
- src/modules/identity/application/quota.ts: consume() agora roda um loop com ate 20 tentativas, re-lendo e tentando updateConditional a cada iteracao, so escrevendo se count/resetAt nao mudaram desde a leitura - se outro consumo concorrente venceu a escrita, tenta de novo com estado fresco.
- test/unit/identity/quota.test.ts ganhou um teste novo de regressao: 25 chamadas concorrentes de consume() com limit=10 - antes do fix isso deixava mais de 10 passarem (lost update); apos o fix, exatamente 10 passam e 15 sao rejeitadas.
- Suite completa roda limpa: typecheck, lint, check-boundaries, npm test (137/137, era 136/136 antes do teste novo).

## `reviews/` â€” convenÃ§Ã£o de nomenclatura e classificaÃ§Ã£o

   - `full-audit-round1-<eixo>-codex-prompt.txt` / `-codex-prompt-round2.txt`, ... â€” prompt enviado ao Codex CLI, um por rodada.
   - `full-audit-round1-<eixo>-codex-output-round1.txt`, `-round2.txt`, ... â€” saÃ­da bruta do Codex CLI. Alguns trechos que ecoam saÃ­da de outros comandos de shell continham mojibake (encoding double/triple-decoded em algumas invocaÃ§Ãµes via PowerShell) â€” revertido deterministicamente para os arquivos anteriores a cada rodada (achado real de higiene, corrigido, nÃ£o sÃ³ mitigado); a saÃ­da de uma rodada em andamento pode ainda conter mojibake atÃ© a rodada seguinte revisÃ¡-la, por ser evidÃªncia datada do que o Codex realmente viu naquele momento â€” nunca editada retroativamente. Se precisar do conteÃºdo, prefira o `-summary.md` correspondente, que jÃ¡ extrai a informaÃ§Ã£o sem o ruÃ­do de encoding.
   - `full-audit-round1-<eixo>-summary.md` â€” **o Ãºnico arquivo desta trinca que deveria ser lido diretamente.** Nota final por critÃ©rio (ambos os lados), achados corrigidos com commit real, achados restantes classificados em "impedimento externo real" vs. "escopo maior que correÃ§Ã£o pontual".

Arquivos de transcript bruto (`.txt`, prefixo `_`, ou os `full-audit-*-codex-output-*.txt`) nunca sÃ£o fonte de verdade por si sÃ³ â€” sempre prefira o `.md` de resumo/nota que os acompanha. **PolÃ­tica de proveniÃªncia/metadata**: os `.md` de nota/resumo carregam frontmatter (`status`/`owner`/`authority`, mais `Last verified` nos routers); os `.txt` brutos nÃ£o â€” exigir frontmatter em cada transcript violaria proporcionalidade (`principles.md` #1) sem ganho real, jÃ¡ que sua proveniÃªncia Ã© herdada do `.md` que os acompanha (mesmo prefixo de nome) e da posiÃ§Ã£o cronolÃ³gica descrita nesta seÃ§Ã£o. Isso Ã© uma decisÃ£o de design, nÃ£o uma lacuna: um transcript nunca Ã© lido isoladamente por convenÃ§Ã£o (ver acima), entÃ£o nÃ£o precisa carregar sua prÃ³pria metadata.

# CritÃ©rios de revisÃ£o conjunta Claudeâ†”Codex, por eixo

## `reviews/` Ã¢â‚¬â€ convenÃƒÂ§ÃƒÂ£o de nomenclatura e classificaÃƒÂ§ÃƒÂ£o

EvidÃƒÂªncia de rodada, mesmo papel que `docs/architecture/history/` tem para decisÃƒÂµes de arquitetura: nunca normativo, sÃƒÂ³ prova de como uma nota/decisÃƒÂ£o foi alcanÃƒÂ§ada. TrÃƒÂªs geraÃƒÂ§ÃƒÂµes de conteÃƒÂºdo coexistem aqui, nesta ordem cronolÃƒÂ³gica:

1. **Checkpoints da Engineering Maturity Review** (`checkpoint-01-rubric/`, `checkpoint-02-09-consolidated/`, `checkpoint-12-redteam/`, e os arquivos soltos `_codex-*-checkpoint1-*.txt` no nÃƒÂ­vel raiz de `reviews/`) Ã¢â‚¬â€ produziram a rubrica CONGELADA (`01-engineering-quality-criteria.md`) e o red team que motivou G8. Prefixo `_` nos arquivos brutos ÃƒÂ© intencional (agrupa antes de outros nomes em ordem alfabÃƒÂ©tica, sinaliza "transcript bruto, nÃƒÂ£o leia direto").
2. **ConvergÃƒÂªncia dos critÃƒÂ©rios por eixo** (`joint-review-criteria-round1-*`, `security-axis-criteria-round1-*`, `remaining-axes-round1-*`, `audit-areas-*`) Ã¢â‚¬â€ produziram `joint-review-criteria.md`.
3. **Full-audit dos 9 eixos formais** (`full-audit-round1-<eixo>-*`) Ã¢â‚¬â€ a rodada em andamento nesta sessÃƒÂ£o. PadrÃƒÂ£o de nomenclatura por eixo (`<eixo>` Ã¢Ë†Ë† `arquitetura`, `qualidade`, `contexto`, `seguranca`, ...):
   - `full-audit-round1-<eixo>-claude.md`, `-claude-round2.md`, `-claude-round3.md`, ... Ã¢â‚¬â€ nota cega do Claude, uma por rodada.
   - `full-audit-round1-<eixo>-codex-prompt.txt` / `-codex-prompt-round2.txt`, ... Ã¢â‚¬â€ prompt enviado ao Codex CLI, um por rodada.
   - `full-audit-round1-<eixo>-codex-output-round1.txt`, `-round2.txt`, ... Ã¢â‚¬â€ saÃƒÂ­da bruta do Codex CLI. Alguns trechos que ecoam saÃƒÂ­da de outros comandos de shell continham mojibake (encoding double/triple-decoded em algumas invocaÃƒÂ§ÃƒÂµes via PowerShell) Ã¢â‚¬â€ revertido deterministicamente para os arquivos anteriores a cada rodada (achado real de higiene, corrigido, nÃƒÂ£o sÃƒÂ³ mitigado); a saÃƒÂ­da de uma rodada em andamento pode ainda conter mojibake atÃƒÂ© a rodada seguinte revisÃƒÂ¡-la, por ser evidÃƒÂªncia datada do que o Codex realmente viu naquele momento Ã¢â‚¬â€ nunca editada retroativamente. Se precisar do conteÃƒÂºdo, prefira o `-summary.md` correspondente, que jÃƒÂ¡ extrai a informaÃƒÂ§ÃƒÂ£o sem o ruÃƒÂ­do de encoding.
   - `full-audit-round1-<eixo>-summary.md` Ã¢â‚¬â€ **o ÃƒÂºnico arquivo desta trinca que deveria ser lido diretamente.** Nota final por critÃƒÂ©rio (ambos os lados), achados corrigidos com commit real, achados restantes classificados em "impedimento externo real" vs. "escopo maior que correÃƒÂ§ÃƒÂ£o pontual".

Arquivos de transcript bruto (`.txt`, prefixo `_`, ou os `full-audit-*-codex-output-*.txt`) nunca sÃƒÂ£o fonte de verdade por si sÃƒÂ³ Ã¢â‚¬â€ sempre prefira o `.md` de resumo/nota que os acompanha. **PolÃƒÂ­tica de proveniÃƒÂªncia/metadata**: os `.md` de nota/resumo carregam frontmatter (`status`/`owner`/`authority`, mais `Last verified` nos routers); os `.txt` brutos nÃƒÂ£o Ã¢â‚¬â€ exigir frontmatter em cada transcript violaria proporcionalidade (`principles.md` #1) sem ganho real, jÃƒÂ¡ que sua proveniÃƒÂªncia ÃƒÂ© herdada do `.md` que os acompanha (mesmo prefixo de nome) e da posiÃƒÂ§ÃƒÂ£o cronolÃƒÂ³gica descrita nesta seÃƒÂ§ÃƒÂ£o. Isso ÃƒÂ© uma decisÃƒÂ£o de design, nÃƒÂ£o uma lacuna: um transcript nunca ÃƒÂ© lido isoladamente por convenÃƒÂ§ÃƒÂ£o (ver acima), entÃƒÂ£o nÃƒÂ£o precisa carregar sua prÃƒÂ³pria metadata.

# CritÃƒÂ©rios de revisÃƒÂ£o conjunta ClaudeÃ¢â€ â€Codex, por eixo

Fonte ÃƒÂºnica dos critÃƒÂ©rios de avaliaÃƒÂ§ÃƒÂ£o (nome, peso, definiÃƒÂ§ÃƒÂ£o) usados nas revisÃƒÂµes conjuntas ClaudeÃ¢â€ â€Codex (`AGENTS.md` Ã‚Â§4). Achados/rodadas de uma execuÃƒÂ§ÃƒÂ£o especÃƒÂ­fica do protocolo (ex. `docs/architecture/reviews/m3.5-runtime-design/`) registram evidÃƒÂªncia de uma revisÃƒÂ£o pontual Ã¢â‚¬â€ nunca redefinem ou duplicam a tabela de pesos; apenas linkam para a seÃƒÂ§ÃƒÂ£o correspondente aqui.

Adaptado (nÃƒÂ£o copiado) do padrÃƒÂ£o equivalente do projeto irmÃƒÂ£o `event-discovery-platform` (mesmo usuÃƒÂ¡rio) Ã¢â‚¬â€ mesmas fontes normativas e mesmos nomes/definiÃƒÂ§ÃƒÂµes de critÃƒÂ©rio (sÃƒÂ£o padrÃƒÂµes de indÃƒÂºstria: ISO/IEC 25010, AWS Well-Architected, ATAM, DORA/Core-4, mais a literatura de context engineering para dev assistido por IA), mas com **pesos recalibrados por convergÃƒÂªncia independente ClaudeÃ¢â€ â€Codex especificamente para este repositÃƒÂ³rio** Ã¢â‚¬â€ nunca herdados sem revisÃƒÂ£o. Rodada de convergÃƒÂªncia completa (2 propostas independentes + reconciliaÃƒÂ§ÃƒÂ£o) em `docs/engineering/reviews/joint-review-criteria-round1-{claude,codex-prompt,codex-output}.*`; ambas as partes Ã¢â€°Â¥9.0 de confianÃƒÂ§a na proposta prÃƒÂ³pria, sem necessidade de rodada formal de desacordo (mesma condiÃƒÂ§ÃƒÂ£o de convergÃƒÂªncia rÃƒÂ¡pida que o projeto irmÃƒÂ£o documenta para o eixo de contexto).

src/modules/reminder/application/reminder-materializer.ts:55:  const inWindow = s <= e ? t >= s && t < e : t >= s || t < e; // handles windows crossing midnight
src/modules/reminder/application/reminder-materializer.ts:56:  if (!inWindow) return { hour, minute };
src/modules/reminder/application/reminder-materializer.ts:57:  return { hour: end.hour, minute: end.minute };

src/modules/reminder/domain/recurrence.ts:5: *
src/modules/reminder/domain/recurrence.ts:6: * No date library dependency (none is in package.json and the blueprint doesn't mandate
src/modules/reminder/domain/recurrence.ts:7: * one) - this uses the standard Intl.DateTimeFormat offset-probing technique (the same
src/modules/reminder/domain/recurrence.ts:8: * core algorithm libraries like date-fns-tz/luxon use under the hood): format a UTC
src/modules/reminder/domain/recurrence.ts:9: * instant back into the target timeZone, compare to the requested wall-clock time, and
src/modules/reminder/domain/recurrence.ts:10: * iterate until the offset stabilizes. Two DST edge cases are handled explicitly:
src/modules/reminder/domain/recurrence.ts:11: *  - AMBIGUOUS (fall-back): the wall-clock time occurs twice. We deterministically pick
src/modules/reminder/domain/recurrence.ts:12: *    the EARLIER UTC instant (the first occurrence), a documented judgment call - the
src/modules/reminder/domain/recurrence.ts:13: *    blueprint requires the case be "covered", not a specific tie-break.
src/modules/reminder/domain/recurrence.ts:14: *  - NONEXISTENT (spring-forward gap): the wall-clock time never occurs. We shift forward
src/modules/reminder/domain/recurrence.ts:15: *    by the size of the gap (typically 60 minutes) to the first valid instant at/after
src/modules/reminder/domain/recurrence.ts:16: *    the requested wall-clock time - never silently drop the reminder.
src/modules/reminder/domain/recurrence.ts:17: */

src/modules/reminder/domain/recurrence.ts:61:/** Offset (minutes) such that: asUtcMillis(localPartsAt(instant)) - instant === offset*60000. */
src/modules/reminder/domain/recurrence.ts:62:function offsetMinutesAt(instantMillis: number, timeZone: string): number {

src/modules/reminder/domain/recurrence.ts:64:  const asUtc = Date.UTC(p.year, p.month - 1, p.day, p.hour, p.minute, p.second ?? 0);
src/modules/reminder/domain/recurrence.ts:65:  return (asUtc - instantMillis) / 60_000;

src/modules/reminder/domain/recurrence.ts:69:  return a.year === b.year && a.month === b.month && a.day === b.day && a.hour === b.hour && a.minute === b.minute;

src/modules/reminder/domain/recurrence.ts:72:/**
src/modules/reminder/domain/recurrence.ts:73: * Resolves a local wall-clock date/time in `timeZone` to a UTC instant.
src/modules/reminder/domain/recurrence.ts:74: * `requested` seconds default to 0.
src/modules/reminder/domain/recurrence.ts:75: */
src/modules/reminder/domain/recurrence.ts:76:export function zonedTimeToUtc(requested: LocalDateTimeParts, timeZone: string): ZonedResolution {
src/modules/reminder/domain/recurrence.ts:77:  const naiveUtc = Date.UTC(
src/modules/reminder/domain/recurrence.ts:78:    requested.year,
src/modules/reminder/domain/recurrence.ts:79:    requested.month - 1,
src/modules/reminder/domain/recurrence.ts:80:    requested.day,
src/modules/reminder/domain/recurrence.ts:81:    requested.hour,
src/modules/reminder/domain/recurrence.ts:82:    requested.minute,
src/modules/reminder/domain/recurrence.ts:83:    requested.second ?? 0,
src/modules/reminder/domain/recurrence.ts:84:  );
src/modules/reminder/domain/recurrence.ts:85:
src/modules/reminder/domain/recurrence.ts:86:  const offset0 = offsetMinutesAt(naiveUtc, timeZone);
src/modules/reminder/domain/recurrence.ts:87:  const guess1 = naiveUtc - offset0 * 60_000;
src/modules/reminder/domain/recurrence.ts:88:  const offset1 = offsetMinutesAt(guess1, timeZone);
src/modules/reminder/domain/recurrence.ts:89:  const guess2 = naiveUtc - offset1 * 60_000;
src/modules/reminder/domain/recurrence.ts:90:  const offset2 = offsetMinutesAt(guess2, timeZone);
src/modules/reminder/domain/recurrence.ts:91:
src/modules/reminder/domain/recurrence.ts:92:  const candidateA = naiveUtc - offset0 * 60_000;
src/modules/reminder/domain/recurrence.ts:93:  const candidateB = naiveUtc - offset1 * 60_000;
src/modules/reminder/domain/recurrence.ts:94:  const localA = formatParts(candidateA, timeZone);
src/modules/reminder/domain/recurrence.ts:95:  const localB = formatParts(candidateB, timeZone);
src/modules/reminder/domain/recurrence.ts:96:  const matchesA = sameWallClock(localA, requested);
src/modules/reminder/domain/recurrence.ts:97:  const matchesB = sameWallClock(localB, requested);
src/modules/reminder/domain/recurrence.ts:98:
src/modules/reminder/domain/recurrence.ts:99:  if (offset1 === offset2 && matchesA && matchesB) {
src/modules/reminder/domain/recurrence.ts:100:    // Both iterations converged to the same instant AND it round-trips - but that alone
src/modules/reminder/domain/recurrence.ts:101:    // doesn't rule out AMBIGUOUS: a fall-back wall clock also round-trips at its first
src/modules/reminder/domain/recurrence.ts:102:    // (pre-transition) instant, since the offset-probing walk naturally lands there first.
src/modules/reminder/domain/recurrence.ts:103:    // Probe a neighboring instant one typical DST-shift (60 min) on the other side of the
src/modules/reminder/domain/recurrence.ts:104:    // transition to check whether the SAME wall clock reoccurs there too (documented
src/modules/reminder/domain/recurrence.ts:105:    // judgment call: 60 min covers every real-world IANA transition this product targets;
src/modules/reminder/domain/recurrence.ts:106:    // sub-hour shifts are historical/rare and out of scope for M3).
src/modules/reminder/domain/recurrence.ts:107:    const probeLater = candidateA + 60 * 60_000;
src/modules/reminder/domain/recurrence.ts:108:    const probeEarlier = candidateA - 60 * 60_000;
src/modules/reminder/domain/recurrence.ts:109:    const laterMatches = sameWallClock(formatParts(probeLater, timeZone), requested);
src/modules/reminder/domain/recurrence.ts:110:    const earlierMatches = sameWallClock(formatParts(probeEarlier, timeZone), requested);
src/modules/reminder/domain/recurrence.ts:111:    if (laterMatches) return { utcMillis: Math.min(candidateA, probeLater), kind: "AMBIGUOUS" };
src/modules/reminder/domain/recurrence.ts:112:    if (earlierMatches) return { utcMillis: Math.min(candidateA, probeEarlier), kind: "AMBIGUOUS" };
src/modules/reminder/domain/recurrence.ts:113:    return { utcMillis: guess2, kind: "NORMAL" };
src/modules/reminder/domain/recurrence.ts:114:  }
src/modules/reminder/domain/recurrence.ts:115:
src/modules/reminder/domain/recurrence.ts:116:  if (matchesA && matchesB) {
src/modules/reminder/domain/recurrence.ts:117:    // Fall-back: two UTC instants produce the same wall clock. Deterministic tie-break:
src/modules/reminder/domain/recurrence.ts:118:    // earlier instant (first occurrence).
src/modules/reminder/domain/recurrence.ts:119:    return { utcMillis: Math.min(candidateA, candidateB), kind: "AMBIGUOUS" };
src/modules/reminder/domain/recurrence.ts:120:  }
src/modules/reminder/domain/recurrence.ts:121:  if (matchesA) return { utcMillis: candidateA, kind: "NORMAL" };
src/modules/reminder/domain/recurrence.ts:122:  if (matchesB) return { utcMillis: candidateB, kind: "NORMAL" };
src/modules/reminder/domain/recurrence.ts:123:
src/modules/reminder/domain/recurrence.ts:124:  // Spring-forward gap: neither candidate reproduces the requested wall clock because it
src/modules/reminder/domain/recurrence.ts:125:  // never occurred. Resolve to the first valid instant at/after the requested wall clock,
src/modules/reminder/domain/recurrence.ts:126:  // by applying the offset that is in effect AFTER the transition (candidateB, which used
src/modules/reminder/domain/recurrence.ts:127:  // offset1 - probed slightly closer to the post-transition regime).
src/modules/reminder/domain/recurrence.ts:128:  return { utcMillis: Math.max(candidateA, candidateB), kind: "NONEXISTENT" };
src/modules/reminder/domain/recurrence.ts:129:}
src/modules/reminder/domain/recurrence.ts:130:

src/modules/reminder/domain/recurrence.ts:162:}
src/modules/reminder/domain/recurrence.ts:163:
src/modules/reminder/domain/recurrence.ts:164:/**
src/modules/reminder/domain/recurrence.ts:165: * M3.5 (docs/architecture/m3.5-runtime-design.md Â§"ReconciliaÃ§Ã£o"; broadened after Codex's
src/modules/reminder/domain/recurrence.ts:166: * implementation review found the original AMBIGUOUS/NONEXISTENT-only trigger too narrow):
src/modules/reminder/domain/recurrence.ts:167: * true if `timeZone` observes DST at all during `year` - compares the UTC offset at two
src/modules/reminder/domain/recurrence.ts:168: * fixed reference instants (Jan 15 and Jul 15 noon UTC), one of which is guaranteed to fall
src/modules/reminder/domain/recurrence.ts:169: * in each side of a DST transition for any zone that has one. A fixed-offset zone (e.g.
src/modules/reminder/domain/recurrence.ts:170: * America/Sao_Paulo since 2019, or any UTC+N zone) always returns false. Used to flag EVERY
src/modules/reminder/domain/recurrence.ts:171: * occurrence in a DST-observing zone as a reconciliation candidate, not just the literally
src/modules/reminder/domain/recurrence.ts:172: * ambiguous/nonexistent ones - a plain 09:00 reminder can still need re-evaluation after an
src/modules/reminder/domain/recurrence.ts:173: * offset change even though its own computed instant was never ambiguous.
src/modules/reminder/domain/recurrence.ts:174: */
src/modules/reminder/domain/recurrence.ts:175:export function timeZoneObservesDst(timeZone: string, year: number): boolean {
src/modules/reminder/domain/recurrence.ts:176:  // Codex implementation review (round 2): comparing only Jan/Jul is not sufficient -
src/modules/reminder/domain/recurrence.ts:177:  // zones with short/irregular DST windows (e.g. Africa/Casablanca, suspended around
src/modules/reminder/domain/recurrence.ts:178:  // Ramadan) can have equal offsets in January and July while still transitioning
src/modules/reminder/domain/recurrence.ts:179:  // elsewhere in the year. Sample the 15th of every month instead - still not a
src/modules/reminder/domain/recurrence.ts:180:  // mathematical proof against a transition entirely contained within a single
src/modules/reminder/domain/recurrence.ts:181:  // sampling gap (<1 month), but IANA DST transitions are structurally at least ~1
src/modules/reminder/domain/recurrence.ts:182:  // month apart in every zone this system targets, so monthly sampling catches every
src/modules/reminder/domain/recurrence.ts:183:  // real-world case without needing a full day-by-day scan.
src/modules/reminder/domain/recurrence.ts:184:  const offsets = new Set<number>();
src/modules/reminder/domain/recurrence.ts:185:  for (let month = 0; month < 12; month++) {
src/modules/reminder/domain/recurrence.ts:186:    offsets.add(offsetMinutesAt(Date.UTC(year, month, 15, 12, 0, 0), timeZone));
src/modules/reminder/domain/recurrence.ts:187:  }
src/modules/reminder/domain/recurrence.ts:188:  return offsets.size > 1;
src/modules/reminder/domain/recurrence.ts:189:}

infra-terraform/modules/reminder-schedule/main.tf:1:# EventBridge Scheduler wiring â€” Terraform equivalent of infra/lib/reminder-schedule.ts.
infra-terraform/modules/reminder-schedule/main.tf:2:#
infra-terraform/modules/reminder-schedule/main.tf:3:# CRITICAL CONTRACT (bug found by a previous Codex implementation review of the CDK code,
infra-terraform/modules/reminder-schedule/main.tf:4:# not reintroduced here): EventBridge Scheduler's Lambda target does NOT wrap the payload in
infra-terraform/modules/reminder-schedule/main.tf:5:# a `detail` envelope the way legacy EventBridge Rules do â€” whatever `input` is set to
infra-terraform/modules/reminder-schedule/main.tf:6:# becomes the event object handed to the Lambda directly. Every schedule below sets an
infra-terraform/modules/reminder-schedule/main.tf:7:# explicit top-level `input` matching exactly what its handler reads (`event.mode`,
infra-terraform/modules/reminder-schedule/main.tf:8:# `event.scheduledTime` â€” see reminder-producer-handler.ts / reminder-reconciliation-
infra-terraform/modules/reminder-schedule/main.tf:9:# handler.ts), never `event.detail.*` / `event.time` (those are Rule-shaped fields that
infra-terraform/modules/reminder-schedule/main.tf:10:# don't exist here). `<aws.scheduler.scheduled-time>` is a Scheduler context attribute,
infra-terraform/modules/reminder-schedule/main.tf:11:# substituted at invoke time with the schedule's fire time (ISO-8601 UTC).

src/modules/identity/application/quota.ts:58:   * backoff/jitter for this fixed-window primitive's expected contention level; exhausting it

src/modules/identity/application/quota.ts:60:   */

src/modules/identity/application/quota.ts:70:      if (!existing) {

src/modules/identity/application/quota.ts:79:          resetAt,
src/modules/identity/application/quota.ts:80:        });
src/modules/identity/application/quota.ts:81:        if (created) return;
src/modules/identity/application/quota.ts:82:        // Lost the create race; loop to re-read the record another caller just created.
src/modules/identity/application/quota.ts:83:        continue;
src/modules/identity/application/quota.ts:84:      }
src/modules/identity/application/quota.ts:85:

src/modules/identity/application/quota.ts:88:      }
src/modules/identity/application/quota.ts:89:
src/modules/identity/application/quota.ts:90:      const windowExpired = existing.resetAt < nowIso;
src/modules/identity/application/quota.ts:91:      const effectiveCount = windowExpired ? 0 : existing.count;
src/modules/identity/application/quota.ts:92:
src/modules/identity/application/quota.ts:93:      if (effectiveCount >= existing.limit) {
src/modules/identity/application/quota.ts:94:        throw new QuotaExceededError("Quota exceeded.", {
src/modules/identity/application/quota.ts:95:          tenantId: input.tenantId,

src/modules/identity/application/quota.ts:97:          limit: existing.limit,
src/modules/identity/application/quota.ts:98:        });
src/modules/identity/application/quota.ts:99:      }
src/modules/identity/application/quota.ts:100:
src/modules/identity/application/quota.ts:101:      const nextResetAt = windowExpired ? resetAt : existing.resetAt;
src/modules/identity/application/quota.ts:102:      const wrote = await this.store.updateConditional(
src/modules/identity/application/quota.ts:103:        { ...existing, count: effectiveCount + 1, resetAt: nextResetAt },
src/modules/identity/application/quota.ts:104:        { count: existing.count, resetAt: existing.resetAt },

src/modules/identity/application/quota.ts:106:      if (wrote) return;
src/modules/identity/application/quota.ts:107:      // Another concurrent consume() won the write race; re-read and retry against fresh state.
src/modules/identity/application/quota.ts:108:    }
src/modules/identity/application/quota.ts:109:

**Nota cega — Critério 3: 7,8/10.**

A race de lost-update foi eliminada: `consume()` relê o estado e usa CAS sobre `count/resetAt`, repetindo após conflito ([quota.ts](C:/Users/Usuario/Desktop/projects/expiration-tracker/src/modules/identity/application/quota.ts:63)); o adapter aplica `ConditionExpression` real e trata falha condicional sem sobrescrever o concorrente ([dynamodb-identity-store.ts](C:/Users/Usuario/Desktop/projects/expiration-tracker/src/modules/identity/persistence/dynamodb-identity-store.ts:55)). O fake reproduz essa semântica ([in-memory-store.ts](C:/Users/Usuario/Desktop/projects/expiration-tracker/test/unit/identity/in-memory-store.ts:32)), e o teste concorrente comprova exatamente 10 sucessos em 25 consumos com limite 10 ([quota.test.ts](C:/Users/Usuario/Desktop/projects/expiration-tracker/test/unit/identity/quota.test.ts:64)).

Permanece abaixo de 9 porque `window`, `limit` e `windowSeconds` ainda são fornecidos pelo chamador ([quota.ts](C:/Users/Usuario/Desktop/projects/expiration-tracker/src/modules/identity/application/quota.ts:29)), sem catálogo comercial centralizado e versionado, plano de billing ou resolução governada de entitlements — lacuna diretamente exigida pelo critério ([joint-review-criteria.md](C:/Users/Usuario/Desktop/projects/expiration-tracker/docs/engineering/joint-review-criteria.md:159)).
